1900.1 NEWCHWANG. CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWCHWANG, 14TH DECEMBER 1900. SIR, I.-The Imperial Russian Provisional Civil Administration having opened, on the 12th August, a Custom House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Likin on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistics of that trade from ist October to 31st December 1900. On all cargoes arriving or leaving by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were collected at Treaty Tariff rates, and Likin at the rate of 1 per cent. ad valoremn on Imports and . per cent. ad valorem on Exports. Boats trading up river paid Tonnage Dues, but no Duties were levied on their cargoes. II. TONNAGE.--The number and movements of junks, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Table:- OcTonn. NOvEMBER. DECEMBER. TOTAL. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended December 31, 1900.
222 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.--DEC., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 352. CHEFOO DISTRICT. FLAGSTAFF POINT LIGHT. TH, following Notice to the Fleet, issued by Sir EDWARD SEYMOUR, K.C.B., Commander in Chief of Her Britannic Majesty&#39;s Naval Forces in Chinese Waters, concerning the new light on Flagstaff Point, is hereby circulated for general information:- &quot;Centurion, off Taku, &quot;I7th October i900. &quot;HYDROGRAPHICAL MEMORANDUM No. 21. WEIHAIWEL &quot;The new illuminating apparatus, which has been established at Flagstaff Point, is fourth order, group-occulting, showing white sectors over the same arcs as the old light. &quot;The light will be eclipsed twice in every 14 seconds-thus, 2 seconds dark, 2 seconds light, 2 seconds dark, 8 seconds light, and so on. &quot;The new lighthouse stands on the site of the old one. &quot;The light is elevated 53 feet above the level of the sea, and in clear weather the white light should be visible 12 miles and the red 8 miles. &quot;The tower and dwellings are painted white. &quot;(Signed) E. H. SEYMOUR, &quot;Vice-Admiral.&quot; SHANGHAI, 27th October iooo.
1900.]. NOTIFICATIONS. 223 No. 353. TIENTSIN AND OHMEF0 DISTRICTS. HOWKI ISLAND AND TSAO-VEI-TIEN ISLAND LIGHTS. NOTICE is hereby given that the above-named lights will not be discontinued during the winter season 190Q-oi. SHANGHAI, 27th October 1900. No. 354. TIENTSIK DISTRICT. SHANHAIKWAN TEMPORARY LIGHT. THis light was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 8th instant. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, of the sixth.order, showing a fixed white light. The lighthouse is placed on the outer bastion of the Great Wall, immediately below the pagoda on the No. I Fort; and the light, which is elevated 84 feet above sea level, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of io nautical miles. The light is hoisted on a mast 38 feet in height. The mast and lightkeepers hut and the parapet of the bastion are painted white. Approximate position:- Latitude.... ................39 58&#39; N. Longitude . . . . . . ... .. . 119* 48&#39; 45&quot; E. SHANGHAI, 22nctNovember 1900.
224 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [oCT.-DEC., 1900.] No. 355. TIENTSIN DISTRICT. CHINWANGTAO TEMPORARY LIGHT. THis light was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 15th instant. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, group-occulting, of the sixth order, showing a fixed white light varied by two eclipses every 20 seconds, thus:- Light . . ............. 14 seconds, Eclipse....... . . . . . . . 2 Light........ .............. 2 Eclipse . . . . . . . .. . . . . 2 ,, and so on. The lighthouse is placed near the south-western extremity of the bluff at Chinwangtao; and the light, which is elevated 87 feet &#39;above sea level, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of io nautical miles. The light is hoisted on a mast 38 feet in height. The mast and lightkeepers hut aie painted white. Approximate position Latitude.............. .... 390 54 5&quot; N. Longitude . . . . . . . . . . ... 1190 38&#39; E. SHANGHAI, 22nd November i9oo.

APPENDIX 1. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. OOTOBER-DECEMBER 1900.
238 CUSTOMS GAZET&#39;T&#39;E. [OCT.-DEC., (Received 16th January i901.) CUSTOM HO1SE, KOWLOON, 8TH JANUARY 1901. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year ioo, October-December. b. The Custom House (M a N) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (k.2ft 30 and HkTb 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April I887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at &#39;the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations then controlled by the Kowloon Customs were known as Capsuimoon Changchow (:r-, Fotochow ( ), and Kowloon (I f). On the, 5th October 1899, as a result of the extension of territory to form part of the Colony of Hongkong, they were removed to Taishan Lintin and Shamchun ( JilJ), on the north and west; to Samun ( on the east; and to Shachung littoral. The Custom House is now called IL Oa e. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; frow China. II. 248 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. LOCT.-DE G, (Received 18th January 190.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, I I1r1 JANUARY 1901. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter of the year 9oo, October-December. b. The Custom House (A C 9) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (L.T&amp;amp; 3o and Hk.2Th 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at.the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Vicerty of the Two Kwang, com- inenced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced On the 1st July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (,g ), Tungho (A $), and Chiensban (&#39;f 1J) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok ( A), Kuttai (W *), and Kwanchiap (N ) sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
